   222	LOG_DIR="${HOME}/.claude/session-state/observed"
   223	MAX_BYTES=5242880               # advisory: ~5 MB across the whole directory
   224	MAX_FILES=2000                  # advisory: a killed worker can leave a 0-byte
   225	                                # file, which adds 0 bytes but 1 inode forever
   226	MAX_INPUT=65536                 # a hook event is a few KB; 1 MB was absurd
   227	DEADLINE=2                      # seconds of wall clock for the worker
   228	
   229	# /usr/bin/python3 IS NOT AN INTERPRETER. On macOS it is the xcrun dispatcher —
   230	# verified 2026-08-31: /usr/bin/python3, /usr/bin/git and /usr/bin/clang are all
   231	# the SAME inode, and DEVELOPER_DIR redirects which real tool it runs. Pinning it
   232	# pinned a dispatcher, not a binary, so the environment still chose the code. The
   233	# real interpreter it dispatches to is listed first below, and the variables that
   234	# steer the dispatcher are removed from the environment before anything runs.
   235	builtin unset DEVELOPER_DIR SDKROOT TOOLCHAINS XCODE_DEVELOPER_DIR_PATH
   236	# The CLT python3 pinned below is NOT a SIP platform binary (codesign flags=0x0,
   237	# no hardened runtime), so unlike /bin/bash and /usr/bin/python3 dyld does NOT
   238	# strip DYLD_* for it, and -I -S -B does not block dylib injection. Today the
   239	# launcher is a SIP /bin/bash whose environment already had these removed, so
   240	# this is latent — which is exactly what the shebang defect looked like before
   241	# settings.json turned out to bypass it. Closing it here does not depend on how
   242	# this file is invoked.
   243	# The WHOLE namespace, not a name list (round 12): a seven-name unset left
   244	# DYLD_VERSIONED_LIBRARY_PATH, DYLD_OVERLAY_PATH and DYLD_PRINT_TO_FILE alive,
   245	# and DYLD_PRINT_TO_FILE makes dyld APPEND its log onto a caller-chosen
   246	# existing file — in-place corruption, which contract 2 exists to prevent.
   247	# ${!DYLD_@} enumerates every DYLD_-prefixed variable bash can see, so a dyld
   248	# version that grows a new variable is covered without editing this file.
   249	for _v in ${!DYLD_@}; do builtin unset "$_v"; done
   250	builtin unset _v
   251	builtin export OBS_DIR="$LOG_DIR" OBS_MAX="$MAX_BYTES" OBS_MAXFILES="$MAX_FILES" \
   252	       OBS_MAXIN="$MAX_INPUT" OBS_MATCHER="${1:-unset}"
   253	
   254	# OBS_CODE MUST be cleared first. If the heredoc redirection below fails — bash
   255	# stages a here-document in a temporary file — the `read` builtin never runs, the
   256	# `|| true` swallows the failure, and an INHERITED OBS_CODE from the environment
   257	# survives into `python3 -c` at the bottom of this file. Reproduced 2026-08-29
   258	# with a failing redirection: OBS_CODE kept its attacker-supplied value and rc
   259	# was 0. The sentinel check after the heredoc is the second half of that fix.
   260	builtin unset OBS_CODE
   261	
   262	# NO HERE-DOCUMENT. bash 3.2 stages a here-doc by creating /tmp/sh-thd-*,
   263	# reopening it BY PATHNAME and then unlinking that pathname — the same
   264	# open-then-unlink-a-name-you-no-longer-own class that was removed from the write
   265	# path in v7, except here it is bash doing it, in the parent, before the watchdog
   266	# exists. A single-quoted assignment needs no temporary file at all. The worker
   267	# body below therefore contains NO apostrophe; `bash -n` catches it if one
   268	# returns.
   269	OBS_CODE='#OBSERVE-WORKER-V9
   270	import sys
   271	# Belt and braces behind `-I`: nothing on sys.path may be the cwd. Claude Code
   272	# runs hooks in the PROJECT directory, and a project file named json.py / re.py /
   273	# fcntl.py executed arbitrary code inside the previous version of this hook on
   274	# every session start (reproduced 2026-08-29). `import sys` is builtin and cannot
   275	# be shadowed, so this runs before any other import.
   276	sys.path[:] = [p for p in sys.path if p not in ("", ".")]
   277	import json, os, select, stat, time
   278	
   279	# Round 13/14: the launcher may leak descriptors above 2 that are not
   280	# CLOEXEC, and an abandoned worker wedged in a filesystem syscall would keep
   281	# holding them — including a pipe some caller is draining to EOF. Everything
   282	# this worker needs is fd 0 (stdin), 1 and 2 (/dev/null); drop the rest
   283	# first. Round 14: a fixed 4096 ceiling left a leaked fd 5000 OPEN.
   284	# Round 15 killed SC_OPEN_MAX as the ceiling both ways (reproduced): it is
   285	# the CURRENT soft limit, so under `ulimit -n unlimited` Darwin returns
   286	# LONG_MAX and closerange raises OverflowError (every event silently lost),
   287	# and after a launcher LOWERS the limit an fd opened under the old high
   288	# limit sits ABOVE the new ceiling and survives. Ground truth is the
   289	# kernel: /dev/fd lists the fds this process actually holds, so close that
   290	# snapshot. closerange with a clamped ceiling remains only as the fallback
   291	# when /dev/fd itself cannot be read, and is wrapped so no value it is
   292	# handed can crash the worker. Neither covers the bash probe window before
   293	# exec, which is documented at the launch site.
   294	_fds = None
   295	try:
   296	    _fds = sorted(int(_n) for _n in os.listdir("/dev/fd"))
   297	except (OSError, ValueError):
   298	    _fds = None
   299	if _fds is not None:
   300	    for _fd in _fds:
   301	        if _fd > 2:
   302	            try:
   303	                os.close(_fd)
   304	            except OSError:
   305	                pass
   306	else:
   307	    try:
   308	        _hi = os.sysconf("SC_OPEN_MAX")
   309	    except (ValueError, OSError, AttributeError):
   310	        _hi = 4096
   311	    if not isinstance(_hi, int) or _hi < 64 or _hi > 1048576:
   312	        _hi = 1048576 if (isinstance(_hi, int) and _hi > 1048576) else 4096
   313	    try:
   314	        os.closerange(3, _hi)
   315	    except (OverflowError, OSError, ValueError):
   316	        pass
   317	
   318	MAXIN = int(os.environ["OBS_MAXIN"])
   319	BUDGET = 1.0
   320	DIAG = []
   321	
   322	# Bounded HERE, before anything can put it in a message. v6 sanitised the matcher
   323	# for the record but interpolated the RAW environment value into the "empty stdin"
   324	# diagnostic, so a 100,000-character matcher produced a 100KB diagnostic file —
   325	# a sequential path straight past MAX_BYTES with no concurrency involved.
   326	ALLOWED = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ0123456789._-"
   327	matcher = "".join(c if c in ALLOWED else "_"
   328	                  for c in os.environ.get("OBS_MATCHER", "unset"))[:64] or "unset"
   329	
   330	def note(m):
   331	    DIAG.append(m)
   332	
   333	# Diagnostics go to a FILE, never to stderr. The worker can outlive this parent
   334	# (see the watchdog), and a stderr it still holds is the Claude Code capture pipe —
   335	# a reader waiting for EOF on that pipe would wait for the abandoned worker,
   336	# defeating the whole point of abandoning it.
   337	def flush_diag(stem):
   338	    """True only if the WHOLE diagnostic reached disk. v5 swallowed EROFS /
   339	    ENOSPC / EACCES here and still exited 0, so a read-only observed/ produced
   340	    no record, no message and success (reproduced by the reviewer)."""
   341	    if not DIAG:
   342	        return True
   343	    body = ("\n".join("observe-compaction: " + m for m in DIAG)
   344	            + "\n").encode("utf-8", "backslashreplace")
   345	    try:
   346	        fd = os.open(stem + ".error.txt",
   347	                     os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)
   348	    except OSError:
   349	        return False
   350	    # The event fd got its guarded close in v11 and THIS one did not — the same
   351	    # defect, one call site away. Reproduced 2026-08-31 with an injected EIO on
   352	    # the diagnostic close: the queued `return True` was discarded, the OSError
   353	    # escaped flush_diag, CPython exited 1, and the parent announced "event NOT
   354	    # recorded" while BOTH the truncated .complete.json and the .error.txt sat
   355	    # on disk. A close that fails means the diagnostic did not land, so it must
   356	    # return False, not raise.
   357	    ok = False
   358	    try:
   359	        ok = os.write(fd, body) == len(body)
   360	    except OSError:
   361	        ok = False
   362	    finally:
   363	        try:
   364	            os.close(fd)
   365	        except OSError:
   366	            ok = False
   367	    return ok
   368	
   369	STEM = [None]
   370	
   371	def die(m, code=0):
   372	    """Diagnostic goes to a file. If it cannot, exit nonzero anyway: since v13
   373	    the parent reaps the code silently (stderr is closed), but the code is kept
   374	    as a machine-readable status for tests and any future phase that reads it."""
   375	    note(m)
   376	    if STEM[0] and flush_diag(STEM[0]):
   377	        sys.exit(code)
   378	    # The diagnostic did NOT reach disk. Code 16 implies a .error.txt exists to
   379	    # be read later; using it here would point at a file that does not exist.
   380	    # 15 is the code that already means "event state unknown, diagnostic did not
   381	    # reach disk" — it deliberately asserts nothing about the file existing.
   382	    if code == 16:
   383	        sys.exit(15)
   384	    sys.exit(code or 13)
   385	
   386	def die_nofile(code):
   387	    """Refuse WITHOUT touching the directory. A cap refusal that writes an
   388	    .error.txt raises the very count it is enforcing: the v5 MAX_FILES=2 run
   389	    left four files, the diagnostics reading "at 2 files" then "at 3 files", and
   390	    every later event added another. Sequential unbounded growth, inside the
   391	    cap that exists to prevent it. The code names the refusal; no file traces it."""
   392	    sys.exit(code)
   393	
   394	# Read BYTES (os.read), never sys.stdin.read(N) — that bounds CHARACTERS, so one
   395	# emoji counted as 1 against the cap while costing 4 bytes. Keep draining after
   396	# the cap: stopping early gives the PRODUCER (Claude Code) EPIPE, and the
   397	# installed binary turns EPIPE during hook input delivery into hook status 1.
   398	raw = bytearray()
   399	truncated = False
   400	saw_eof = False
   401	timed_out = False
   402	read_error = ""
   403	t0 = time.monotonic()
   404	while True:
   405	    left = BUDGET - (time.monotonic() - t0)
   406	    if left <= 0:
   407	        timed_out = True
   408	        break
   409	    try:
   410	        r, _, _ = select.select([0], [], [], left)
   411	    except OSError as e:
   412	        read_error = "select %s" % e.errno
   413	        break
   414	    if not r:
   415	        timed_out = True
   416	        break
   417	    try:
   418	        chunk = os.read(0, 65536)
   419	    except OSError as e:
   420	        read_error = "read %s" % e.errno
   421	        break
   422	    if not chunk:
   423	        saw_eof = True            # the ONLY place completeness may be claimed
   424	        break
   425	    room = MAXIN - len(raw)
   426	    if room > 0:
   427	        raw.extend(chunk[:room])
   428	    if len(chunk) > room:
   429	        truncated = True          # exact: os.read does not over-read like head(1)
   430	
   431	# Close stdin BEFORE any filesystem call. After this the watchdog can kill us
   432	# without the producer ever seeing EPIPE.
   433	try:
   434	    os.close(0)
   435	except OSError:
   436	    pass
   437	
   438	# The directory and the stem must exist BEFORE the first die(), or its diagnostic
   439	# has nowhere to go: worker stderr is /dev/null, so the v5 first draft dropped the
   440	# "empty stdin" message entirely — silently. Reproduced 2026-08-29 (U7: zero
   441	# files, zero diagnostics, rc 0).
   442	d = os.environ["OBS_DIR"]
   443	try:
   444	    os.makedirs(d, 0o700)
   445	except FileExistsError:
   446	    pass
   447	except OSError:
   448	    die_nofile(10)
   449	
   450	# ADVISORY caps (contract clause 3), checked HERE — before STEM exists, so no
   451	# refusal path can add a file to the directory it is capping. Bytes alone are not
   452	# enough: a worker killed between O_EXCL create and its first write leaves a
   453	# ZERO-byte file, which adds no bytes and can repeat forever, so inodes are
   454	# capped too. The reserve is 2: one event file plus one possible .error.txt, the
   455	# most any single run can create. Neither bound is hard under concurrency: this is a check-then-act.
   456	# The reserve covers the record plus one possible .error.txt, so the precise size
   457	# is not needed yet and the check can run this early.
   458	total = 0
   459	count = 0
   460	try:
   461	    for e in os.scandir(d):
   462	        count += 1
   463	        try:
   464	            total += e.stat().st_size
   465	        except OSError:
   466	            pass
   467	except OSError:
   468	    die_nofile(14)
   469	if count + 2 > int(os.environ["OBS_MAXFILES"]):
   470	    die_nofile(11)
   471	# 6x, not 1x. ensure_ascii escapes one NUL byte to the six characters \u0000, so
   472	# 65,536 NULs serialise to a 393,331-byte record — v6 reserved 66,560 and would
   473	# have sailed past MAX_BYTES sequentially. Measured on this exact serialiser.
   474	if total + 6 * MAXIN + 8192 > int(os.environ["OBS_MAX"]):
   475	    die_nofile(12)
   476	
   477	STEM[0] = os.path.join(d, "%d-%d" % (time.time_ns(), os.getpid()))
   478	
   479	# Order matters: a read that produced nothing is not an empty event when it timed
   480	# out or errored, and reporting it as one hides a hung producer behind a routine
   481	# message.
   482	if not raw:
   483	    if timed_out:
   484	        die("stdin still open with no data after the read budget — event NOT recorded")
   485	    if read_error:
   486	        die("stdin %s before any data — event NOT recorded" % read_error)
   487	    die("empty stdin for matcher %s — event NOT recorded" % matcher)
   488	
   489	try:
   490	    txt = raw.decode("utf-8")
   491	except UnicodeDecodeError:
   492	    txt = raw.decode("utf-8", "backslashreplace")
   493	txt = txt.rstrip("\r\n")
   494	
   495	# The payload is NEVER json.loads-ed here. Parsing it would turn NaN / Infinity /
   496	# 1e999 in the input into bare NaN / Infinity in our OUTPUT, which strict parsers
   497	# reject (reproduced). `raw` stays a string; allow_nan=False guards the wrapper.
   498	rec = json.dumps({
   499	    "observed_at": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()),
   500	    "registered_matcher": matcher,
   501	    "truncated": truncated,
   502	    "saw_eof": saw_eof,
   503	    "raw": txt,
   504	}, ensure_ascii=True, allow_nan=False) + "\n"
   505	data = rec.encode("utf-8")
   506	
   507	stem = STEM[0]
   508	
   509	# WRITE STRAIGHT TO THE FINAL NAME. v6 wrote a .part and published with
   510	# link + unlink, but that unlink resolves a PATHNAME: another same-UID writer can
   511	# remove our .part and create its own file at that name in the window between the
   512	# link and the unlink, and we then delete THEIR data. O_EXCL cannot protect a
   513	# later path lookup. The hook now never unlinks anything, so contract clause 2 is
   514	# literal rather than nearly true.
   515	#
   516	# The cost, stated plainly: a worker killed mid-write leaves a TRUNCATED final
   517	# file instead of an ignorable .part. That is survivable — the record ends with
   518	# "}\n", so a short write is not parseable JSON and the reader reports it
   519	# UNREADABLE — whereas deleting another writer file is not survivable.
   520	if not saw_eof:
   521	    suffix = ".partial.json"      # EOF never observed: NOT a whole event
   522	elif truncated:
   523	    suffix = ".truncated.json"
   524	else:
   525	    suffix = ".complete.json"
   526	final = stem + suffix
   527	flags = os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_NOFOLLOW
   528	if hasattr(os, "O_CLOEXEC"):
   529	    flags |= os.O_CLOEXEC
   530	try:
   531	    fd = os.open(final, flags, 0o600)
   532	except OSError as e:
   533	    die("cannot create event file — event NOT recorded (%s)" % e.errno)
   534	# wrote_ok carries the short-write fact PAST this block. v10 only note()d it,
   535	# and note() alone reaches exit 0 whenever flush_diag succeeds: nothing then
   536	# recorded that a file named .complete.json held a truncated record. Measured
   537	# on v10 under ulimit -f 1: a 1024-byte .complete.json that raises
   538	# JSONDecodeError. The suffix is chosen before the write and the no-unlink
   539	# rule forbids renaming afterwards, so the durable remedy is the SHORT WRITE
   540	# line note() puts into the .error.txt. Exit 17 remains as a machine-readable
   541	# status for the parent, which reaps it silently since v13.
   542	wrote_ok = True
   543	try:
   544	    st = os.fstat(fd)
   545	    if not stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode) or st.st_nlink != 1:
   546	        # 16, not 15: v8 reused 15 here, whose parent message asserts the
   547	        # diagnostic could not be written — false whenever it WAS written. 16
   548	        # says only that the file was refused. st_nlink can also be 0 (another
   549	        # writer removed the name), so this does not claim a husk remains.
   550	        die("event file was not freshly created (nlink=%d) — not written"
   551	            % st.st_nlink, 16)
   552	    w = os.write(fd, data)
   553	    if w != len(data):
   554	        wrote_ok = False
   555	        note("SHORT WRITE %d of %d — the file is truncated and will not parse"
   556	             % (w, len(data)))
   557	except OSError as e:
   558	    # ENOSPC, EFBIG, EIO and friends. v10 let these escape the worker entirely:
   559	    # an uncaught OSError exits CPython 1, so the parent said "worker exited 1 —
   560	    # event NOT recorded" while a .complete.json sat on disk holding a partial
   561	    # record. Same lie as the short write, reached by a different route.
   562	    wrote_ok = False
   563	    note("WRITE FAILED errno %s — the file is truncated and will not parse"
   564	         % e.errno)
   565	finally:
   566	    # v11 swallowed this and in the same motion HID the failure: NFS and FUSE
   567	    # report a deferred write error on close, not on write, so os.write could
   568	    # return the full count, close could fail, wrote_ok stayed True and the
   569	    # parent said nothing about a file named .complete.json. Reproduced
   570	    # 2026-08-31 with an injected EIO: worker exit 0, no diagnostic, no stderr.
   571	    # It still must not raise — that was the v11 lesson — so it clears the flag.
   572	    try:
   573	        os.close(fd)
   574	    except OSError as e:
   575	        wrote_ok = False
   576	        note("CLOSE FAILED errno %s — the file may not have reached disk"
   577	             % e.errno)
   578	
   579	if timed_out:
   580	    note("stdin still open at the read budget — record is .partial")
   581	if read_error:
   582	    note("stdin %s — record is .partial" % read_error)
   583	# v6 threw this Boolean away, so a diagnostic that could not be written after a
   584	# SHORT WRITE reached exit 0 and the parent said nothing at all. Code 15 is
   585	# deliberately NOT 13: by this point an event file exists, so "event NOT
   586	# recorded" would be a lie.
   587	if not flush_diag(stem):
   588	    sys.exit(15)
   589	# Order matters: 15 (no diagnostic at all) outranks 17 (diagnostic written, file
   590	# truncated), because 15 is the case where the .error.txt cannot be read later.
   591	if not wrote_ok:
   592	    sys.exit(17)
   593	'
   594	
   595	# Second half of the D13 fix: prove the heredoc actually produced OUR code before
   596	# handing it to an interpreter. An inherited or truncated OBS_CODE fails here.
   597	case "$OBS_CODE" in
   598	  "#OBSERVE-WORKER-V9"*) builtin : ;;
   599	  *) builtin exit 0 ;;   # silent by design: stderr is closed, and no worker ran, so
   600	                 # there is no .error.txt either. The event is lost — cheap.
   601	esac
   602	
   603	# `exec 3<&0` + `<&3` is LOAD-BEARING. bash redirects a background job's stdin
   604	# from /dev/null when job control is off, which it is in a hook — without this the
   605	# worker reads ZERO bytes and reports success on a perfectly normal event.
   606	# Reproduced twice on 2026-08-29, once in my own draft and once in a reviewer's.
   607	# stderr goes to /dev/null for the reason given at flush_diag() above.
   608	exec 3<&0
   609	# The parent drops its OWN stdin now: fd 3 already holds the caller's event for
   610	# the worker, and every fork the parent makes during the probe below (the poll
   611	# `/bin/sleep`, each probe subshell) must NOT inherit the caller's pipe. A probe
   612	# child abandoned mid-startup therefore cannot hold caller stdin. v26 nested the
   613	# probe inside the worker and closed fd 3 there with `3<&-`; the probe now runs
   614	# one level up, so the parent closes its fd 0 here and each probe child re-closes
   615	# fd 3 explicitly.
   616	exec 0</dev/null
   617	
   618	# ROUND 27 (codex-sol + grok review of v26; H1 reproduced 2026-09-01): the
   619	# interpreter self-test moved OUT of the worker subshell and UP into the parent.
   620	# v26 ran the probe as a GRANDCHILD (a child of the worker). The top-of-file trap
   621	# runs `builtin kill -KILL $(builtin jobs -pr)`, and `jobs -pr` lists only the
   622	# parent's OWN jobs -- so a terminating signal delivered PID-directed to the hook
   623	# while a probe hung killed the worker ($wid) and ABANDONED the hung probe: a
   624	# runnable stub (`while :; do :; done`) reparented to launchd and span at 100% CPU
   625	# with no deadline left, because the inner 1s killer lived inside the worker that
   626	# just died. Reproduced under H01+SIGTERM@0.35s: v26 left 1 spinning survivor,
   627	# v22 left 2. The comment v26 shipped ("window at most the 1s probe budget") was
   628	# false -- the orphan's lifetime is unbounded. The fix reconciles two invariants
   629	# that pull against each other:
   630	#   (a) ROUND 12 -- the `[ -x ]` stat(2) must NOT run in the parent: a wedged
   631	#       automount would block the parent with a terminating signal deferred
   632	#       inside the syscall and no watchdog yet alive. So each candidate's stat
   633	#       AND probe run inside a background subshell `( ... )`.
   634	#   (b) H1 -- the probe must be a DIRECT child of the parent so the trap reaches
   635	#       it. `builtin exec "$_p"` at the tail of that subshell REPLACES the
   636	#       subshell with the interpreter, so `$!` IS the interpreter pid and it is a
   637	#       job of the PARENT: `jobs -pr` lists it and the top-of-file trap kills it.
   638	# NO process-group change anywhere (keeps the approved v22 delivery; answers the
   639	# withdrawn v24's F1/F2). The probe body, the exit-37 success channel, the shared
   640	# 1s budget and the pid-directed inner kill are UNCHANGED from v26 -- only the
   641	# process that owns them moved up one level. The worker below is now a bare `exec`
   642	# of the chosen interpreter: no poll loop and no `/bin/sleep` run in it, so no
   643	# foreground grandchild can inherit caller stdin (this also closes v26's M1 nit).
   644	#
   645	# Candidates are ROOT-OWNED absolute paths only (round 15): /opt/homebrew/bin and
   646	# /usr/local/bin are same-UID-writable, and this probe is a skip-broken-file
   647	# check, NOT an authentication -- ANY program that exits 37 is then exec-ed with
   648	# this environment. `-x` alone is TRUE for a directory, so `-f` pins each
   649	# candidate to a regular file. If no candidate passes, no worker is spawned and
   650	# the event is silently lost -- priced cheap by the contract.
   651	#
   652	# The shared 1s budget is set ONCE before the loop. SECONDS is whole-second
   653	# granularity, so the budget expires at the next integer tick -- between (0,1]s
   654	# after this assignment depending on the sub-second phase when it runs (v26's
   655	# comment claimed a 0.05s lower bound; corrected here per the L1 review nit). A
   656	# slow-but-honest first interpreter whose startup crosses that tick is killed and
   657	# the event lost: load/FS-dependent event loss, which the contract prices cheap.
   658	# The C3 bound still holds: the whole probe phase is < the outer DEADLINE=2.
   659	_chosen=""
   660	_pend=$(( SECONDS + 1 ))
   661	for _p in /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/usr/bin/python3 \
   662	          /usr/bin/python3; do
   663	  # Budget already spent by an earlier candidate: do not even spawn (the wedged
   664	  # first-candidate host prices as event loss, round 26).
   665	  if builtin [ "$SECONDS" -ge "$_pend" ]; then builtin break; fi
   666	  # `( stat && exec python )` -- see (a)/(b) above. `</dev/null` so the probe
   667	  # cannot eat event bytes; `3<&-` so an abandoned probe cannot hold the caller's
   668	  # fd-3 event dup during python startup (before the body's own closerange runs);
   669	  # stdout/stderr to /dev/null. Probe body is byte-identical to v26: it closes
   670	  # inherited fds >2 then exits 37 on python >= 3.7 (a 3.6 that would die on
   671	  # time.time_ns at every event is version-gated OUT here), 0 otherwise; an
   672	  # ENOEXEC/corrupt candidate exits != 37 and is skipped.
   673	  (
   674	    if builtin [ -x "$_p" ] && builtin [ -f "$_p" ]; then
   675	      builtin exec "$_p" -I -S -B -c 'import os, sys
   676	try:
   677	    _fds = sorted(int(_n) for _n in os.listdir("/dev/fd"))
   678	except Exception:
   679	    _fds = None
   680	if _fds is not None:
   681	    for _fd in _fds:
   682	        if _fd > 2:
   683	            try:
   684	                os.close(_fd)
   685	            except OSError:
   686	                pass
   687	else:
   688	    try:
   689	        _hi = os.sysconf("SC_OPEN_MAX")
   690	    except Exception:
   691	        _hi = 4096
   692	    if not isinstance(_hi, int) or _hi < 64 or _hi > 1048576:
   693	        _hi = 1048576 if (isinstance(_hi, int) and _hi > 1048576) else 4096
   694	    try:
   695	        os.closerange(3, _hi)
   696	    except Exception:
   697	        pass
   698	sys.exit(37 if sys.version_info >= (3, 7) else 0)'
   699	    fi
   700	    builtin exit 1
   701	  ) </dev/null 3<&- >/dev/null 2>/dev/null &
   702	  _ppid=$!
   703	  _pkilled=no
   704	  while builtin :; do
   705	    _prun=$(builtin jobs -pr)
   706	    case "
   707	$_prun
   708	" in
   709	      *"
   710	$_ppid
   711	"*) builtin : ;;
   712	      *) builtin break ;;
   713	    esac
   714	    if builtin [ "$SECONDS" -ge "$_pend" ]; then
   715	      builtin kill -KILL "$_ppid" 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   716	      _pkilled=yes
   717	      builtin break
   718	    fi
   719	    /bin/sleep 0.05 </dev/null >/dev/null 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   720	  done
   721	  _prc=137
   722	  if builtin [ "$_pkilled" = no ]; then
   723	    if builtin wait "$_ppid" 2>/dev/null; then _prc=0; else _prc=$?; fi
   724	  fi
   725	  if builtin [ "$_prc" -eq 37 ]; then _chosen="$_p"; builtin break; fi
   726	done
   727	
   728	# WORKER: only if a candidate passed. A bare exec of the chosen interpreter with
   729	# the event on fd 0 (via `<&3`). `3<&-` closes the parent's event dup inside the
   730	# worker after it becomes fd 0, so an abandoned worker's own os.close(0) frees the
   731	# producer's pipe (round-13 rationale, unchanged).
   732	if builtin [ -n "$_chosen" ]; then
   733	  (
   734	    builtin exec "$_chosen" -I -S -B -c "$OBS_CODE"
   735	  ) <&3 3<&- >/dev/null 2>/dev/null &
   736	  wid=$!
   737	else
   738	  wid=""
   739	fi
   740	exec 3<&-
   741	
   742	# No interpreter passed the probe: no worker exists to bound. The event is
   743	# already lost (cheap); skip the watchdog entirely -- an empty $wid would make
   744	# the poll `case` below match a blank line and mis-detect "finished".
   745	if builtin [ -z "$wid" ]; then builtin exit 0; fi
   746	
   747	# Poll with a BUILTIN, never by forking /bin/ps. A synchronous ps runs BEFORE the
   748	# shell can look at its own deadline, and Apple ps retries a failing process-table
   749	# sysctl for ~1000s, so the watchdog could outlast the thing it bounds. `jobs -pr`
   750	# reads bash memory: it cannot block, cannot fail open, and needs no fork.
   751	# Verified 2026-08-31 in a non-interactive script with job control off: a live
   752	# child is listed, a completed one is NOT, and `wait` on the completed one
   753	# returned its saved status (7) in 0s — so the exit-code channel survives.
   754	#
   755	# A worker wedged in an uninterruptible syscall is still "running", so it stays
   756	# listed, we reach the deadline, and we kill and abandon it. That is the intent.
   757	#
   758	# NO EXTERNAL COMMAND HERE. v9 wrote `jobs -pr | grep -q`, and `grep` resolves
   759	# through the INHERITED PATH. Measured 2026-08-31: with PATH=/nonexistent, grep
   760	# exited 127, `!` inverted that into the "worker finished" branch on the FIRST
   761	# iteration, and the parent fell into an unbounded `wait` — a worker forced to
   762	# outlive DEADLINE=2 held the hook for its full 10s, and the deadline never
   763	# fired. A wedged worker would have wedged the hook forever, silently: stdout
   764	# stayed empty and the exit stayed 0, so nothing else would have flagged it.
   765	# `jobs`, `wait` and `kill` are all bash BUILTINS — but round 14 proved a
   766	# builtin NAME is still FUNCTION-dispatched first under a non -p invocation:
   767	# an exported jobs() printing nothing blinded this loop (the deadline kill
   768	# was never reached) and an exported kill() swallowed the deadline signal,
   769	# orphaning the worker with Claude stdin. All three are `builtin`-forced
   770	# below; PATH was never the only dispatch channel. The case below reproduces
   771	# grep's anchoring: the newline guards make a pid that is a substring of a live
   772	# pid (2923 vs 29235) fail to match, which `^...$` was there to guarantee.
   773	end=$(( SECONDS + DEADLINE ))
   774	while builtin :; do
   775	  running=$(builtin jobs -pr)
   776	  case "
   777	$running
   778	" in
   779	    *"
   780	$wid
   781	"*) builtin : ;;             # still running — fall through to the deadline check
   782	    *)
   783	    # Gone from the job table means bash already reaped it and holds its
   784	    # status, so this wait returns immediately. The status is REAPED AND
   785	    # DISCARDED: stderr is closed (see the top of this file), so the parent
   786	    # has nowhere to put a message and deliberately says nothing. The worker
   787	    # exit codes still exist (10-17 documented at the worker, 18 = no
   788	    # interpreter) and every one that can leave a durable trace already wrote
   789	    # a .error.txt before exiting; codes 13, 15 and 18 are the ones that
   790	    # cannot, and those events are silently lost — priced cheap by contract.
   791	    builtin wait "$wid" 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   792	    builtin break
   793	    ;;
   794	  esac
   795	  if builtin [ "$SECONDS" -ge "$end" ]; then
   796	    # Abandon without wait: a worker wedged in an uninterruptible syscall
   797	    # cannot complete SIGKILL, and a wait here would hang the parent on it.
   798	    # The event file, if one was opened, may be left truncated; the reader
   799	    # reports such a file as UNREADABLE.
   800	    builtin kill -KILL "$wid" 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   801	    builtin break
   802	  fi
   803	  # `|| true` IS LOAD-BEARING. The trap at the top of this file catches ERR, and
   804	  # with no `set -e` a BARE failing command still fires it: a /bin/sleep that
   805	  # ever returned non-zero would run `exit 0` here and abandon a live worker
   806	  # without reaching the deadline kill. Verified 2026-08-31: bare fails, guarded
   807	  # continues. Every other command in this loop is already inside if/case/||.
   808	  /bin/sleep 0.05 || builtin true
   809	done
   810	builtin exit 0
   811	

--- V22 NUMBERED ---
     1	#!/bin/bash -p
     2	# PHASE 0 — MEASUREMENT ONLY. v22, one file per event. NOT INSTALLED.
     3	#
     4	# WHAT IT DOES: writes ONE file per hook event into $LOG_DIR/observed/, named
     5	#   <ns>-<pid>.complete.json   EOF was OBSERVED and nothing was discarded
     6	#   <ns>-<pid>.truncated.json  EOF observed, but more bytes existed than MAX_INPUT
     7	#   <ns>-<pid>.partial.json    EOF was NEVER observed (read timed out or errored)
     8	#   (there is no .part file: v7 writes straight to the final name, because the
     9	#    unlink that published a .part could delete another writer's file — see the
    10	#    write path. A worker killed mid-write therefore leaves a TRUNCATED .json,
    11	#    which is not parseable and which the reader reports as UNREADABLE.)
    12	#   <ns>-<pid>.error.txt       a diagnostic; the event was NOT recorded
    13	# Each .json is one JSON object: observed_at, registered_matcher, truncated,
    14	# saw_eof, raw. `raw` is the received text, decoded UTF-8, trailing CR/LF
    15	# stripped. It is NOT byte-identical to stdin and does not claim to be.
    16	#
    17	# ONLY .complete.json IS A WHOLE EVENT. v4 labelled a timed-out partial read
    18	# `.complete.json` with truncated:false — reproduced 2026-08-29 with 13 bytes and
    19	# a held-open pipe. The stderr note said "may be incomplete" but the FILE, which
    20	# is the only thing a reader ever sees, claimed otherwise. saw_eof now decides.
    21	#
    22	# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: no shared log, no lock, no parse of the payload on the
    23	# write path, no ftruncate, no unlink of anything it did not create this run,
    24	# no rotation, no stdout on any path, and — since v13 — no stderr either: both
    25	# are closed to /dev/null at the top of this file. Every diagnostic lives in a
    26	# .error.txt; when even that cannot be written the event is silently lost,
    27	# which the contract prices as CHEAP.
    28	#
    29	# WHY ONE FILE PER EVENT (v1-v3 were all rejected; this is why): a shared
    30	# appended JSONL created most of the defect surface at once — a blocking flock
    31	# hung the hook, a non-blocking one dropped 18.8% of close-pair events, a torn
    32	# line killed the documented reader for the WHOLE log, an ftruncate rollback
    33	# destroyed another writer's committed record, and O_NOFOLLOW does not refuse a
    34	# hardlink to that one inode. None of those exist when each event is its own file.
    35	#
    36	# CONTRACT:
    37	#  1. FAIL OPEN. Exit 0 and zero bytes on stdout on EVERY path. PreCompact can
    38	#     BLOCK compaction; SessionStart stdout enters the model's context window.
    39	#  2. NEVER DELETES AND NEVER REPLACES ANY FILE. There is no unlink, no rename,
    40	#     no ftruncate anywhere in this hook. Every file is created with O_EXCL, so
    41	#     an existing name makes the create fail instead of clobbering it. v4 used a
    42	#     replacing rename; v5-v6 published a .part with link + unlink, and that
    43	#     unlink resolves a pathname another writer can have taken over.
    44	#  3. Bounded in bytes per event (MAX_INPUT) and in TIME (the worker deadline).
    45	#     THE DIRECTORY CAPS ARE ADVISORY, NOT HARD: MAX_BYTES and MAX_FILES are a
    46	#     check-then-act against a directory other processes are writing, so N
    47	#     concurrent workers can each pass the check and overshoot by up to N records.
    48	#     A hard bound needs a lock, and a lock is what made v1-v3 unshippable.
    49	#  4. D10 IS NARROWED, ON PURPOSE: the time bound holds on a responsive local
    50	#     filesystem. A worker wedged in an uninterruptible syscall on a network or
    51	#     FUSE $HOME cannot be killed by any userspace watchdog; this parent abandons
    52	#     it and still exits 0, so the HOOK is bounded even when the worker is not.
    53	#     Claiming more than that would be false. Since v13 the interpreter probe
    54	#     also runs INSIDE the watched child (round 12: a parent-side stat of an
    55	#     automounted path had no time bound at all). The parent still execs
    56	#     /bin/sleep every 50ms in the watchdog (a standing, adjudicated
    57	#     trade-off: a builtin busy-poll burns CPU on every event and a same-UID
    58	#     STOP can freeze the parent either way) and /dev/null; those are the
    59	#     parent-side filesystem touches, stated plainly.
    60	#
    61	# INVOCATION — THE SHEBANG CANNOT SAVE YOU HERE.
    62	# LAUNCHER-OWNED, NOT FIXABLE IN THIS FILE (round 13, reproduced): with
    63	# RLIMIT_NOFILE forced to ~4-12 by the launcher, the `exec >/dev/null`
    64	# below cannot get a descriptor and BASH ITSELF writes redirect-failure
    65	# diagnostics to the still-inherited stderr before any line of this file
    66	# has control — on a full pipe that is an indefinite hang, and at limit 3
    67	# bash aborts (rc 134). No trap or redirect written HERE runs early enough.
    68	# The same class: SHELLOPTS=xtrace writes trace lines to inherited stderr
    69	# before the redirect under a non -p invocation — and with BASH_XTRACEFD=1
    70	# the same trace lands on STDOUT (round 15, reproduced: `+ exec` on fd 1
    71	# before line 121 runs), a direct contract-2 byte. Both are properties of
    72	# the invoking configuration, listed here so they are not rediscovered. The live settings.json runs
    73	# `bash <path> <matcher>`, and an explicit `bash` IGNORES the shebang: measured
    74	# 2026-08-31, a BASH_ENV payload RAN under that form and was ignored under both
    75	# `./<path>` and the -p form below. Installing this file without also changing
    76	# settings.json leaves the code-execution path open.
    77	# settings.json must call this as:
    78	#   /bin/bash --noprofile --norc -p <path-to-this-file> <matcher>
    79	# FLAG ORDER MATTERS on this bash 3.2: `-p` must come AFTER the GNU long
    80	# options. `/bin/bash -p --noprofile ...` is rc 2 plus a usage dump to the
    81	# inherited stderr BEFORE this file runs (reproduced round 14) — a launcher
    82	# footgun that violates contract 1 (rc 2) and the zero-stderr promise by
    83	# itself (stdout stays clean; contract 2 is not the one it breaks).
    84	# The shebang on line 1 ALSO carries -p, because a settings.json entry that names
    85	# the script by path executes it through that shebang instead, and a plain
    86	# `#!/bin/bash` there would undo the whole defence. Verified 2026-08-30: with
    87	# `#!/bin/bash` a BASH_ENV payload RAN; with `#!/bin/bash -p` it did not.
    88	# `-p` is load-bearing, not tidiness: without it a non-interactive bash sources
    89	# $BASH_ENV before line 1 of this script and imports exported shell functions,
    90	# which is the same arbitrary-code-execution shape as the python3 defect below.
    91	# Verified 2026-08-29: BASH_ENV ran under `bash`, was ignored under `bash -p`.
    92	#
    93	# READ THE RESULTS WITH:
    94	#   /usr/bin/python3 -I -S -c '
    95	#   import json,os
    96	#   d=os.path.expanduser("~/.claude/session-state/observed")
    97	#   for n in sorted(os.listdir(d)):
    98	#     if not n.endswith(".json"): continue
    99	#     try: r=json.loads(open(os.path.join(d,n)).read())
   100	#     except Exception: print((n,"UNREADABLE")); continue
   101	#     p={}
   102	#     if r.get("saw_eof") and not r.get("truncated"):
   103	#       try: p=json.loads(r.get("raw") or "")
   104	#       except Exception: p={}
   105	#     if not isinstance(p,dict): p={}
   106	#     print((n.split(".")[-2],p.get("hook_event_name"),p.get("session_id"),
   107	#            repr(p.get("cwd")),r.get("registered_matcher")))'
   108	# The legacy ~/.claude/session-state/observed.jsonl (20 records, 4 of them
   109	# synthetic from broken test harnesses of mine) is NOT migrated and NOT deleted.
   110	
   111	# BOTH stdout and stderr are closed before anything else runs. stdout is
   112	# contract 1. stderr is round 11 + round 12, one lesson in two halves:
   113	#   round 11: a synchronous foreground `printf >&2` onto an already-full
   114	#     65536-byte pipe blocked this hook indefinitely, and bash defers traps
   115	#     while a foreground write is in progress, so TERM did not help.
   116	#   round 12: the fix — a BACKGROUNDED write with a kill budget (emit) — was
   117	#     itself broken on the signal path: TERM during the poll made the parent
   118	#     exit 0 while the blocked writer was reparented to PID 1 STILL HOLDING
   119	#     the stderr pipe, so a caller draining stderr to EOF hung instead.
   120	#     Reproduced 5/5 by the round-12 reviewer and again by me.
   121	# Both reviewers converged on the same prescription: there is no safe way to
   122	# write to an arbitrary inherited fd 2 under a hard time bound, so do not
   123	# write to it at all. Diagnostics live in the .error.txt files the worker
   124	# writes; when those cannot be written the message is LOST, which the
   125	# contract prices as cheap. A hung or hanging-on caller is the expensive case.
   126	# `exec` here CANNOT take the `builtin` prefix: bash persists exec's
   127	# redirections only when `exec` is the command word itself — under `builtin
   128	# exec >...` the redirection is temporary to the builtin invocation and fd
   129	# state reverts (reproduced in v16 verification: fd 3 was gone one line
   130	# later and stderr leaked). Redirection-only exec stays bare; the exported-
   131	# function exposure of the NAME exec under a non -p invocation is the same
   132	# launcher-owned residue as export -f builtin, listed above.
   133	# ROUND 17 (sol): install a no-output terminating-signal trap BEFORE the very
   134	# first executable command. Line below (the redirect) is that first command;
   135	# a catchable terminating signal (TERM/INT/...) arriving in the microsecond
   136	# window between the redirect and the full trap took DEFAULT disposition and
   137	# exited 143 (contract 1 violation, reproduced rc 143 on bash 3.2). This early
   138	# trap makes `builtin exit 0` the disposition from the first command onward;
   139	# it omits the job-cleanup kill because no worker exists yet. The only window
   140	# left is BEFORE bash runs its first command, which is launcher/process-
   141	# lifecycle territory, not in-file fixable. The full trap below supersedes it
   142	# once the worker can exist (adds ERR + the background-job kill).
   143	# ROUND 18 (sol): both traps cover EVERY catchable default-terminating
   144	# signal on Darwin, not just TERM/INT — an untrapped USR1/ALRM/XCPU/etc.
   145	# took its default disposition and exited nonzero (rc 158 from USR1,
   146	# reproduced), violating contract 1. KILL and STOP cannot be trapped and
   147	# stay launcher-owned. Fault signals (SEGV/BUS/...) are near-unreachable for
   148	# a small script but are trapped too so no reachable signal path exits nonzero.
   149	# ROUND 19 (sol): TSTP/TTIN/TTOU default to SUSPEND, not terminate. In a
   150	# non-orphaned process group (a hook spawned by a live parent in the same
   151	# session -- the normal launch shape) an untrapped TSTP left the parent
   152	# stopped indefinitely (reproduced via waitpid WUNTRACED), violating
   153	# contract 3. ROUND 20 (sol+grok independently): trapping them to `exit 0`
   154	# (the v20 fix) only covers PID-directed delivery. A trapped signal resets
   155	# to SIG_DFL in children; only an IGNORED one is inherited. So killpg TSTP
   156	# stopped the foreground /bin/sleep and the worker, bash deferred the
   157	# trapped handler until the stopped-forever foreground child returned, and
   158	# the hook blocked indefinitely (reproduced 4/4 timings). `trap ''` is the
   159	# correct disposition: the parent cannot be suspended AND every child
   160	# inherits SIG_IGN, covering both delivery shapes. In an orphaned group
   161	# the kernel discards these anyway; inherited-ignored cannot be re-trapped
   162	# but also cannot suspend. KILL and STOP remain untrappable/launcher-owned.
   163	# ROUND 21 (sol): line 164's `trap ''` only takes effect once it RUNS. Between
   164	# this early exit-0 trap (the FIRST executable line) and line 164, TSTP/TTIN/TTOU
   165	# briefly sit at default-SUSPEND -- an in-file window, distinct from the
   166	# launcher-owned pre-first-line window both reviewers accept as unfixable here.
   167	# A TSTP delivered in that window (PID- or group-directed) suspended the process
   168	# (reproduced STOPPED via a pure-builtin busy-wait; an earlier /bin/sleep marker
   169	# gave a false BLOCKED by itself becoming a killable foreground child). Fix:
   170	# include TSTP/TTIN/TTOU in THIS exit-0 trap too, so the gap is covered; line 164
   171	# then upgrades them to inherited-SIG_IGN for the worker. No foreground child
   172	# exists in the gap, so exit-0 here cannot defer -- the round-20 deferral needs a
   173	# stopped foreground child, and none is spawned until after line 164.
   174	builtin trap 'builtin exit 0' HUP INT QUIT ILL TRAP ABRT EMT FPE BUS SEGV SYS PIPE ALRM TERM XCPU XFSZ VTALRM PROF USR1 USR2 TSTP TTIN TTOU
   175	builtin trap '' TSTP TTIN TTOU
   176	exec >/dev/null 2>/dev/null
   177	# The trap KILLS any background job before exiting (round 12): with a worker
   178	# still in the job table, a bare `exit 0` on TERM abandoned it holding the
   179	# stdin pipe. `jobs -pr` is a builtin; an empty list makes kill fail, which
   180	# `|| true` absorbs so the ERR trap cannot re-enter this handler. XFSZ is
   181	# included because a write past RLIMIT_FSIZE on a regular fd delivers SIGXFSZ
   182	# and killed the round-12 parent with exit 153 — with fd 1/2 on /dev/null the
   183	# trigger is gone, but the trap converts any residual delivery into exit 0.
   184	# `builtin` is round-13/14 defence in depth: under a non -p invocation bash
   185	# imports exported shell functions, and an exported kill() that does not
   186	# signal was shown to win over the builtin — the round-12 orphan, back via
   187	# the trap. Round 14 proved the SAME dispatch bites `jobs`: an exported
   188	# jobs() printing nothing made this kill a no-op (and blinded the watchdog
   189	# below). Round 15 went further: bare `:`, `[` and `|| true` in the watchdog
   190	# were STILL function-dispatched (an exported : hung the hook past its
   191	# deadline, reproduced), so v16 swept builtin names file-wide — and round 16
   192	# caught the name that sweep still missed: bare `break` in the watchdog
   193	# (an exported break() looped the parent forever / exited it rc 1,
   194	# reproduced), so v17 prefixes `break` too. The sweep target is every bash
   195	# builtin in command position, with redirection-only `exec` as the sole
   196	# documented exception (see the exec comment below). That prefix is BEST-EFFORT ONLY: `builtin` is itself a builtin
   197	# NAME, and `export -f builtin` defeats every prefixed site at once
   198	# (round 15, reproduced — rc 41 from an exported builtin()). There is no
   199	# in-file spelling that survives that; only the -p invocation does. The
   200	# prefix narrows the exported-function surface, it does not close it.
   201	builtin trap 'builtin kill -KILL $(builtin jobs -pr) 2>/dev/null || builtin true; builtin exit 0' ERR HUP INT QUIT ILL TRAP ABRT EMT FPE BUS SEGV SYS PIPE ALRM TERM XCPU XFSZ VTALRM PROF USR1 USR2
   202	builtin trap '' TSTP TTIN TTOU
   203	builtin umask 077
   204	
   205	LOG_DIR="${HOME}/.claude/session-state/observed"
   206	MAX_BYTES=5242880               # advisory: ~5 MB across the whole directory
   207	MAX_FILES=2000                  # advisory: a killed worker can leave a 0-byte
   208	                                # file, which adds 0 bytes but 1 inode forever
   209	MAX_INPUT=65536                 # a hook event is a few KB; 1 MB was absurd
   210	DEADLINE=2                      # seconds of wall clock for the worker
   211	
   212	# /usr/bin/python3 IS NOT AN INTERPRETER. On macOS it is the xcrun dispatcher —
   213	# verified 2026-08-31: /usr/bin/python3, /usr/bin/git and /usr/bin/clang are all
   214	# the SAME inode, and DEVELOPER_DIR redirects which real tool it runs. Pinning it
   215	# pinned a dispatcher, not a binary, so the environment still chose the code. The
   216	# real interpreter it dispatches to is listed first below, and the variables that
   217	# steer the dispatcher are removed from the environment before anything runs.
   218	builtin unset DEVELOPER_DIR SDKROOT TOOLCHAINS XCODE_DEVELOPER_DIR_PATH
   219	# The CLT python3 pinned below is NOT a SIP platform binary (codesign flags=0x0,
   220	# no hardened runtime), so unlike /bin/bash and /usr/bin/python3 dyld does NOT
   221	# strip DYLD_* for it, and -I -S -B does not block dylib injection. Today the
   222	# launcher is a SIP /bin/bash whose environment already had these removed, so
   223	# this is latent — which is exactly what the shebang defect looked like before
   224	# settings.json turned out to bypass it. Closing it here does not depend on how
   225	# this file is invoked.
   226	# The WHOLE namespace, not a name list (round 12): a seven-name unset left
   227	# DYLD_VERSIONED_LIBRARY_PATH, DYLD_OVERLAY_PATH and DYLD_PRINT_TO_FILE alive,
   228	# and DYLD_PRINT_TO_FILE makes dyld APPEND its log onto a caller-chosen
   229	# existing file — in-place corruption, which contract 2 exists to prevent.
   230	# ${!DYLD_@} enumerates every DYLD_-prefixed variable bash can see, so a dyld
   231	# version that grows a new variable is covered without editing this file.
   232	for _v in ${!DYLD_@}; do builtin unset "$_v"; done
   233	builtin unset _v
   234	builtin export OBS_DIR="$LOG_DIR" OBS_MAX="$MAX_BYTES" OBS_MAXFILES="$MAX_FILES" \
   235	       OBS_MAXIN="$MAX_INPUT" OBS_MATCHER="${1:-unset}"
   236	
   237	# OBS_CODE MUST be cleared first. If the heredoc redirection below fails — bash
   238	# stages a here-document in a temporary file — the `read` builtin never runs, the
   239	# `|| true` swallows the failure, and an INHERITED OBS_CODE from the environment
   240	# survives into `python3 -c` at the bottom of this file. Reproduced 2026-08-29
   241	# with a failing redirection: OBS_CODE kept its attacker-supplied value and rc
   242	# was 0. The sentinel check after the heredoc is the second half of that fix.
   243	builtin unset OBS_CODE
   244	
   245	# NO HERE-DOCUMENT. bash 3.2 stages a here-doc by creating /tmp/sh-thd-*,
   246	# reopening it BY PATHNAME and then unlinking that pathname — the same
   247	# open-then-unlink-a-name-you-no-longer-own class that was removed from the write
   248	# path in v7, except here it is bash doing it, in the parent, before the watchdog
   249	# exists. A single-quoted assignment needs no temporary file at all. The worker
   250	# body below therefore contains NO apostrophe; `bash -n` catches it if one
   251	# returns.
   252	OBS_CODE='#OBSERVE-WORKER-V9
   253	import sys
   254	# Belt and braces behind `-I`: nothing on sys.path may be the cwd. Claude Code
   255	# runs hooks in the PROJECT directory, and a project file named json.py / re.py /
   256	# fcntl.py executed arbitrary code inside the previous version of this hook on
   257	# every session start (reproduced 2026-08-29). `import sys` is builtin and cannot
   258	# be shadowed, so this runs before any other import.
   259	sys.path[:] = [p for p in sys.path if p not in ("", ".")]
   260	import json, os, select, stat, time
   261	
   262	# Round 13/14: the launcher may leak descriptors above 2 that are not
   263	# CLOEXEC, and an abandoned worker wedged in a filesystem syscall would keep
   264	# holding them — including a pipe some caller is draining to EOF. Everything
   265	# this worker needs is fd 0 (stdin), 1 and 2 (/dev/null); drop the rest
   266	# first. Round 14: a fixed 4096 ceiling left a leaked fd 5000 OPEN.
   267	# Round 15 killed SC_OPEN_MAX as the ceiling both ways (reproduced): it is
   268	# the CURRENT soft limit, so under `ulimit -n unlimited` Darwin returns
   269	# LONG_MAX and closerange raises OverflowError (every event silently lost),
   270	# and after a launcher LOWERS the limit an fd opened under the old high
   271	# limit sits ABOVE the new ceiling and survives. Ground truth is the
   272	# kernel: /dev/fd lists the fds this process actually holds, so close that
   273	# snapshot. closerange with a clamped ceiling remains only as the fallback
   274	# when /dev/fd itself cannot be read, and is wrapped so no value it is
   275	# handed can crash the worker. Neither covers the bash probe window before
   276	# exec, which is documented at the launch site.
   277	_fds = None
   278	try:
   279	    _fds = sorted(int(_n) for _n in os.listdir("/dev/fd"))
   280	except (OSError, ValueError):
   281	    _fds = None
   282	if _fds is not None:
   283	    for _fd in _fds:
   284	        if _fd > 2:
   285	            try:
   286	                os.close(_fd)
   287	            except OSError:
   288	                pass
   289	else:
   290	    try:
   291	        _hi = os.sysconf("SC_OPEN_MAX")
   292	    except (ValueError, OSError, AttributeError):
   293	        _hi = 4096
   294	    if not isinstance(_hi, int) or _hi < 64 or _hi > 1048576:
   295	        _hi = 1048576 if (isinstance(_hi, int) and _hi > 1048576) else 4096
   296	    try:
   297	        os.closerange(3, _hi)
   298	    except (OverflowError, OSError, ValueError):
   299	        pass
   300	
   301	MAXIN = int(os.environ["OBS_MAXIN"])
   302	BUDGET = 1.0
   303	DIAG = []
   304	
   305	# Bounded HERE, before anything can put it in a message. v6 sanitised the matcher
   306	# for the record but interpolated the RAW environment value into the "empty stdin"
   307	# diagnostic, so a 100,000-character matcher produced a 100KB diagnostic file —
   308	# a sequential path straight past MAX_BYTES with no concurrency involved.
   309	ALLOWED = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ0123456789._-"
   310	matcher = "".join(c if c in ALLOWED else "_"
   311	                  for c in os.environ.get("OBS_MATCHER", "unset"))[:64] or "unset"
   312	
   313	def note(m):
   314	    DIAG.append(m)
   315	
   316	# Diagnostics go to a FILE, never to stderr. The worker can outlive this parent
   317	# (see the watchdog), and a stderr it still holds is the Claude Code capture pipe —
   318	# a reader waiting for EOF on that pipe would wait for the abandoned worker,
   319	# defeating the whole point of abandoning it.
   320	def flush_diag(stem):
   321	    """True only if the WHOLE diagnostic reached disk. v5 swallowed EROFS /
   322	    ENOSPC / EACCES here and still exited 0, so a read-only observed/ produced
   323	    no record, no message and success (reproduced by the reviewer)."""
   324	    if not DIAG:
   325	        return True
   326	    body = ("\n".join("observe-compaction: " + m for m in DIAG)
   327	            + "\n").encode("utf-8", "backslashreplace")
   328	    try:
   329	        fd = os.open(stem + ".error.txt",
   330	                     os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)
   331	    except OSError:
   332	        return False
   333	    # The event fd got its guarded close in v11 and THIS one did not — the same
   334	    # defect, one call site away. Reproduced 2026-08-31 with an injected EIO on
   335	    # the diagnostic close: the queued `return True` was discarded, the OSError
   336	    # escaped flush_diag, CPython exited 1, and the parent announced "event NOT
   337	    # recorded" while BOTH the truncated .complete.json and the .error.txt sat
   338	    # on disk. A close that fails means the diagnostic did not land, so it must
   339	    # return False, not raise.
   340	    ok = False
   341	    try:
   342	        ok = os.write(fd, body) == len(body)
   343	    except OSError:
   344	        ok = False
   345	    finally:
   346	        try:
   347	            os.close(fd)
   348	        except OSError:
   349	            ok = False
   350	    return ok
   351	
   352	STEM = [None]
   353	
   354	def die(m, code=0):
   355	    """Diagnostic goes to a file. If it cannot, exit nonzero anyway: since v13
   356	    the parent reaps the code silently (stderr is closed), but the code is kept
   357	    as a machine-readable status for tests and any future phase that reads it."""
   358	    note(m)
   359	    if STEM[0] and flush_diag(STEM[0]):
   360	        sys.exit(code)
   361	    # The diagnostic did NOT reach disk. Code 16 implies a .error.txt exists to
   362	    # be read later; using it here would point at a file that does not exist.
   363	    # 15 is the code that already means "event state unknown, diagnostic did not
   364	    # reach disk" — it deliberately asserts nothing about the file existing.
   365	    if code == 16:
   366	        sys.exit(15)
   367	    sys.exit(code or 13)
   368	
   369	def die_nofile(code):
   370	    """Refuse WITHOUT touching the directory. A cap refusal that writes an
   371	    .error.txt raises the very count it is enforcing: the v5 MAX_FILES=2 run
   372	    left four files, the diagnostics reading "at 2 files" then "at 3 files", and
   373	    every later event added another. Sequential unbounded growth, inside the
   374	    cap that exists to prevent it. The code names the refusal; no file traces it."""
   375	    sys.exit(code)
   376	
   377	# Read BYTES (os.read), never sys.stdin.read(N) — that bounds CHARACTERS, so one
   378	# emoji counted as 1 against the cap while costing 4 bytes. Keep draining after
   379	# the cap: stopping early gives the PRODUCER (Claude Code) EPIPE, and the
   380	# installed binary turns EPIPE during hook input delivery into hook status 1.
   381	raw = bytearray()
   382	truncated = False
   383	saw_eof = False
   384	timed_out = False
   385	read_error = ""
   386	t0 = time.monotonic()
   387	while True:
   388	    left = BUDGET - (time.monotonic() - t0)
   389	    if left <= 0:
   390	        timed_out = True
   391	        break
   392	    try:
   393	        r, _, _ = select.select([0], [], [], left)
   394	    except OSError as e:
   395	        read_error = "select %s" % e.errno
   396	        break
   397	    if not r:
   398	        timed_out = True
   399	        break
   400	    try:
   401	        chunk = os.read(0, 65536)
   402	    except OSError as e:
   403	        read_error = "read %s" % e.errno
   404	        break
   405	    if not chunk:
   406	        saw_eof = True            # the ONLY place completeness may be claimed
   407	        break
   408	    room = MAXIN - len(raw)
   409	    if room > 0:
   410	        raw.extend(chunk[:room])
   411	    if len(chunk) > room:
   412	        truncated = True          # exact: os.read does not over-read like head(1)
   413	
   414	# Close stdin BEFORE any filesystem call. After this the watchdog can kill us
   415	# without the producer ever seeing EPIPE.
   416	try:
   417	    os.close(0)
   418	except OSError:
   419	    pass
   420	
   421	# The directory and the stem must exist BEFORE the first die(), or its diagnostic
   422	# has nowhere to go: worker stderr is /dev/null, so the v5 first draft dropped the
   423	# "empty stdin" message entirely — silently. Reproduced 2026-08-29 (U7: zero
   424	# files, zero diagnostics, rc 0).
   425	d = os.environ["OBS_DIR"]
   426	try:
   427	    os.makedirs(d, 0o700)
   428	except FileExistsError:
   429	    pass
   430	except OSError:
   431	    die_nofile(10)
   432	
   433	# ADVISORY caps (contract clause 3), checked HERE — before STEM exists, so no
   434	# refusal path can add a file to the directory it is capping. Bytes alone are not
   435	# enough: a worker killed between O_EXCL create and its first write leaves a
   436	# ZERO-byte file, which adds no bytes and can repeat forever, so inodes are
   437	# capped too. The reserve is 2: one event file plus one possible .error.txt, the
   438	# most any single run can create. Neither bound is hard under concurrency: this is a check-then-act.
   439	# The reserve covers the record plus one possible .error.txt, so the precise size
   440	# is not needed yet and the check can run this early.
   441	total = 0
   442	count = 0
   443	try:
   444	    for e in os.scandir(d):
   445	        count += 1
   446	        try:
   447	            total += e.stat().st_size
   448	        except OSError:
   449	            pass
   450	except OSError:
   451	    die_nofile(14)
   452	if count + 2 > int(os.environ["OBS_MAXFILES"]):
   453	    die_nofile(11)
   454	# 6x, not 1x. ensure_ascii escapes one NUL byte to the six characters \u0000, so
   455	# 65,536 NULs serialise to a 393,331-byte record — v6 reserved 66,560 and would
   456	# have sailed past MAX_BYTES sequentially. Measured on this exact serialiser.
   457	if total + 6 * MAXIN + 8192 > int(os.environ["OBS_MAX"]):
   458	    die_nofile(12)
   459	
   460	STEM[0] = os.path.join(d, "%d-%d" % (time.time_ns(), os.getpid()))
   461	
   462	# Order matters: a read that produced nothing is not an empty event when it timed
   463	# out or errored, and reporting it as one hides a hung producer behind a routine
   464	# message.
   465	if not raw:
   466	    if timed_out:
   467	        die("stdin still open with no data after the read budget — event NOT recorded")
   468	    if read_error:
   469	        die("stdin %s before any data — event NOT recorded" % read_error)
   470	    die("empty stdin for matcher %s — event NOT recorded" % matcher)
   471	
   472	try:
   473	    txt = raw.decode("utf-8")
   474	except UnicodeDecodeError:
   475	    txt = raw.decode("utf-8", "backslashreplace")
   476	txt = txt.rstrip("\r\n")
   477	
   478	# The payload is NEVER json.loads-ed here. Parsing it would turn NaN / Infinity /
   479	# 1e999 in the input into bare NaN / Infinity in our OUTPUT, which strict parsers
   480	# reject (reproduced). `raw` stays a string; allow_nan=False guards the wrapper.
   481	rec = json.dumps({
   482	    "observed_at": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()),
   483	    "registered_matcher": matcher,
   484	    "truncated": truncated,
   485	    "saw_eof": saw_eof,
   486	    "raw": txt,
   487	}, ensure_ascii=True, allow_nan=False) + "\n"
   488	data = rec.encode("utf-8")
   489	
   490	stem = STEM[0]
   491	
   492	# WRITE STRAIGHT TO THE FINAL NAME. v6 wrote a .part and published with
   493	# link + unlink, but that unlink resolves a PATHNAME: another same-UID writer can
   494	# remove our .part and create its own file at that name in the window between the
   495	# link and the unlink, and we then delete THEIR data. O_EXCL cannot protect a
   496	# later path lookup. The hook now never unlinks anything, so contract clause 2 is
   497	# literal rather than nearly true.
   498	#
   499	# The cost, stated plainly: a worker killed mid-write leaves a TRUNCATED final
   500	# file instead of an ignorable .part. That is survivable — the record ends with
   501	# "}\n", so a short write is not parseable JSON and the reader reports it
   502	# UNREADABLE — whereas deleting another writer file is not survivable.
   503	if not saw_eof:
   504	    suffix = ".partial.json"      # EOF never observed: NOT a whole event
   505	elif truncated:
   506	    suffix = ".truncated.json"
   507	else:
   508	    suffix = ".complete.json"
   509	final = stem + suffix
   510	flags = os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_NOFOLLOW
   511	if hasattr(os, "O_CLOEXEC"):
   512	    flags |= os.O_CLOEXEC
   513	try:
   514	    fd = os.open(final, flags, 0o600)
   515	except OSError as e:
   516	    die("cannot create event file — event NOT recorded (%s)" % e.errno)
   517	# wrote_ok carries the short-write fact PAST this block. v10 only note()d it,
   518	# and note() alone reaches exit 0 whenever flush_diag succeeds: nothing then
   519	# recorded that a file named .complete.json held a truncated record. Measured
   520	# on v10 under ulimit -f 1: a 1024-byte .complete.json that raises
   521	# JSONDecodeError. The suffix is chosen before the write and the no-unlink
   522	# rule forbids renaming afterwards, so the durable remedy is the SHORT WRITE
   523	# line note() puts into the .error.txt. Exit 17 remains as a machine-readable
   524	# status for the parent, which reaps it silently since v13.
   525	wrote_ok = True
   526	try:
   527	    st = os.fstat(fd)
   528	    if not stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode) or st.st_nlink != 1:
   529	        # 16, not 15: v8 reused 15 here, whose parent message asserts the
   530	        # diagnostic could not be written — false whenever it WAS written. 16
   531	        # says only that the file was refused. st_nlink can also be 0 (another
   532	        # writer removed the name), so this does not claim a husk remains.
   533	        die("event file was not freshly created (nlink=%d) — not written"
   534	            % st.st_nlink, 16)
   535	    w = os.write(fd, data)
   536	    if w != len(data):
   537	        wrote_ok = False
   538	        note("SHORT WRITE %d of %d — the file is truncated and will not parse"
   539	             % (w, len(data)))
   540	except OSError as e:
   541	    # ENOSPC, EFBIG, EIO and friends. v10 let these escape the worker entirely:
   542	    # an uncaught OSError exits CPython 1, so the parent said "worker exited 1 —
   543	    # event NOT recorded" while a .complete.json sat on disk holding a partial
   544	    # record. Same lie as the short write, reached by a different route.
   545	    wrote_ok = False
   546	    note("WRITE FAILED errno %s — the file is truncated and will not parse"
   547	         % e.errno)
   548	finally:
   549	    # v11 swallowed this and in the same motion HID the failure: NFS and FUSE
   550	    # report a deferred write error on close, not on write, so os.write could
   551	    # return the full count, close could fail, wrote_ok stayed True and the
   552	    # parent said nothing about a file named .complete.json. Reproduced
   553	    # 2026-08-31 with an injected EIO: worker exit 0, no diagnostic, no stderr.
   554	    # It still must not raise — that was the v11 lesson — so it clears the flag.
   555	    try:
   556	        os.close(fd)
   557	    except OSError as e:
   558	        wrote_ok = False
   559	        note("CLOSE FAILED errno %s — the file may not have reached disk"
   560	             % e.errno)
   561	
   562	if timed_out:
   563	    note("stdin still open at the read budget — record is .partial")
   564	if read_error:
   565	    note("stdin %s — record is .partial" % read_error)
   566	# v6 threw this Boolean away, so a diagnostic that could not be written after a
   567	# SHORT WRITE reached exit 0 and the parent said nothing at all. Code 15 is
   568	# deliberately NOT 13: by this point an event file exists, so "event NOT
   569	# recorded" would be a lie.
   570	if not flush_diag(stem):
   571	    sys.exit(15)
   572	# Order matters: 15 (no diagnostic at all) outranks 17 (diagnostic written, file
   573	# truncated), because 15 is the case where the .error.txt cannot be read later.
   574	if not wrote_ok:
   575	    sys.exit(17)
   576	'
   577	
   578	# Second half of the D13 fix: prove the heredoc actually produced OUR code before
   579	# handing it to an interpreter. An inherited or truncated OBS_CODE fails here.
   580	case "$OBS_CODE" in
   581	  "#OBSERVE-WORKER-V9"*) builtin : ;;
   582	  *) builtin exit 0 ;;   # silent by design: stderr is closed, and no worker ran, so
   583	                 # there is no .error.txt either. The event is lost — cheap.
   584	esac
   585	
   586	# `exec 3<&0` + `<&3` is LOAD-BEARING. bash redirects a background job's stdin
   587	# from /dev/null when job control is off, which it is in a hook — without this the
   588	# worker reads ZERO bytes and reports success on a perfectly normal event.
   589	# Reproduced twice on 2026-08-29, once in my own draft and once in a reviewer's.
   590	# stderr goes to /dev/null for the reason given at flush_diag() above.
   591	exec 3<&0
   592	# `3<&-` IS LOAD-BEARING. Without it fd 3 stays OPEN in the worker, so the
   593	# worker's own os.close(0) does NOT release the producer's pipe: an abandoned,
   594	# unkillable worker keeps holding it. Reproduced 2026-08-29 — child_fd3=OPEN
   595	# without the close, EBADF with it. The parent then drops its own fd 0 so the
   596	# pipe has no reader left here either.
   597	# The interpreter PROBE runs inside this background subshell, not the parent
   598	# (round 12): `[ -x ]` is a stat(2), and a wedged automount of
   599	# /Library/Developer or /opt/homebrew would have blocked the PARENT with no
   600	# watchdog yet alive and TERM deferred inside the syscall. Here the same wedge
   601	# blocks only this child, which the deadline below kills and abandons.
   602	# HONEST LIMIT (round 13): until the exec, this bash child holds the Claude
   603	# stdin pipe on fd 0 — the worker closes it only after python starts. A child
   604	# abandoned DURING the probe therefore still holds stdin. A caller hangs on
   605	# that only if it is also mid-write on a payload past the pipe buffer; hook
   606	# events are a few KB, so the write completes and the write end closes long
   607	# before the deadline. Stashing the pipe elsewhere does not help — a D-state
   608	# process keeps every copy it has.
   609	# /usr/bin/python3 IS the xcrun dispatcher (same inode as git and clang); the
   610	# CLT interpreter it dispatches to is listed first, and the variables that
   611	# steer the dispatcher were unset above. HOST-CONFIGURATION-OWNED residue
   612	# (round 15): the dispatcher resolves through the active-developer config; a
   613	# host whose privileged xcode-select target points into a user-writable tree
   614	# reopens same-UID exec through this fallback. Two independent probes of the
   615	# env/cache steering surface found no payload with the unsets above in
   616	# place, so the fallback stays; a root config is outside this file. -x alone
   617	# is TRUE for a directory, so -f pins each candidate to a regular file. If
   618	# no candidate exists the child exits 18 and the event is silently lost.
   619	(
   620	  # ROUND 15: candidates are ROOT-OWNED paths only. /opt/homebrew/bin and
   621	  # /usr/local/bin are same-UID-writable on typical macOS hosts, and the
   622	  # probe below is a skip-broken-file check, NOT an authentication — ANY
   623	  # program that prints 1337 passes it and is then exec-ed with this
   624	  # environment (reproduced round 14 with an echo stub). Dropping the two
   625	  # mutable paths trades event loss on brew-only hosts (priced cheap by
   626	  # contract) against same-UID code execution (contract-2 expensive).
   627	  for _p in /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/usr/bin/python3 \
   628	            /usr/bin/python3; do
   629	    # PRE-FLIGHT, round 13: a candidate that passes -x and -f can still fail
   630	    # exec (a zero-byte or corrupted file), and bash 3.2 then EXITS this
   631	    # subshell — with the broken file listed FIRST, every later working
   632	    # interpreter was skipped and every event silently lost, forever.
   633	    # `trap - ERR` does NOT fix that (verified: the subshell still dies on the
   634	    # failed exec), so each candidate must survive an output-checked probe
   635	    # before it is exec-ed. ENOEXEC fallback runs a broken file as an empty
   636	    # shell script, which prints nothing and is skipped. stdin is pinned to
   637	    # /dev/null so the probe cannot eat event bytes.
   638	    # ROUND 15/16, in the probe body: (a) fds 3+ are closed as the FIRST
   639	    # STATEMENT of the probe — but python startup itself (encodings, os.py)
   640	    # reads the filesystem before any user code, so a probe wedged DURING
   641	    # startup still holds inherited fds: the closerange narrows the window,
   642	    # it does not close it (launcher-owned residue, same as below);
   643	    # (b) 1337 is printed only on python >= 3.7 — 3.6 would
   644	    # pass a bare probe, then die on time.time_ns at every event, silently
   645	    # and forever (round-14 sol finding, version-gated here instead).
   646	    # HONEST LIMIT (round 14, reproduced): the deadline kills the WAITER
   647	    # subshell, not this probe child. A probe wedged BEFORE its closerange
   648	    # runs (a non-python stub, or an exec stuck in D-state) is abandoned
   649	    # still holding every inherited non-CLOEXEC launcher descriptor. No
   650	    # in-file code can reach that window — the launcher must not leak fds.
   651	    # LAUNCHER-OWNED residue.
   652	    if builtin [ -x "$_p" ] && builtin [ -f "$_p" ] \
   653	       && builtin [ "$("$_p" -I -S -B -c '
   654	import os, sys
   655	try:
   656	    _fds = sorted(int(_n) for _n in os.listdir("/dev/fd"))
   657	except Exception:
   658	    _fds = None
   659	if _fds is not None:
   660	    for _fd in _fds:
   661	        if _fd > 2:
   662	            try:
   663	                os.close(_fd)
   664	            except OSError:
   665	                pass
   666	else:
   667	    try:
   668	        _hi = os.sysconf("SC_OPEN_MAX")
   669	    except Exception:
   670	        _hi = 4096
   671	    if not isinstance(_hi, int) or _hi < 64 or _hi > 1048576:
   672	        _hi = 1048576 if (isinstance(_hi, int) and _hi > 1048576) else 4096
   673	    try:
   674	        os.closerange(3, _hi)
   675	    except Exception:
   676	        pass
   677	print(7*191 if sys.version_info >= (3, 7) else 0)' </dev/null 2>/dev/null)" = "1337" ]; then
   678	      builtin exec "$_p" -I -S -B -c "$OBS_CODE"
   679	    fi
   680	  done
   681	  builtin exit 18
   682	) <&3 3<&- >/dev/null 2>/dev/null &
   683	wid=$!
   684	exec 3<&-
   685	exec 0</dev/null
   686	
   687	# Poll with a BUILTIN, never by forking /bin/ps. A synchronous ps runs BEFORE the
   688	# shell can look at its own deadline, and Apple ps retries a failing process-table
   689	# sysctl for ~1000s, so the watchdog could outlast the thing it bounds. `jobs -pr`
   690	# reads bash memory: it cannot block, cannot fail open, and needs no fork.
   691	# Verified 2026-08-31 in a non-interactive script with job control off: a live
   692	# child is listed, a completed one is NOT, and `wait` on the completed one
   693	# returned its saved status (7) in 0s — so the exit-code channel survives.
   694	#
   695	# A worker wedged in an uninterruptible syscall is still "running", so it stays
   696	# listed, we reach the deadline, and we kill and abandon it. That is the intent.
   697	#
   698	# NO EXTERNAL COMMAND HERE. v9 wrote `jobs -pr | grep -q`, and `grep` resolves
   699	# through the INHERITED PATH. Measured 2026-08-31: with PATH=/nonexistent, grep
   700	# exited 127, `!` inverted that into the "worker finished" branch on the FIRST
   701	# iteration, and the parent fell into an unbounded `wait` — a worker forced to
   702	# outlive DEADLINE=2 held the hook for its full 10s, and the deadline never
   703	# fired. A wedged worker would have wedged the hook forever, silently: stdout
   704	# stayed empty and the exit stayed 0, so nothing else would have flagged it.
   705	# `jobs`, `wait` and `kill` are all bash BUILTINS — but round 14 proved a
   706	# builtin NAME is still FUNCTION-dispatched first under a non -p invocation:
   707	# an exported jobs() printing nothing blinded this loop (the deadline kill
   708	# was never reached) and an exported kill() swallowed the deadline signal,
   709	# orphaning the worker with Claude stdin. All three are `builtin`-forced
   710	# below; PATH was never the only dispatch channel. The case below reproduces
   711	# grep's anchoring: the newline guards make a pid that is a substring of a live
   712	# pid (2923 vs 29235) fail to match, which `^...$` was there to guarantee.
   713	end=$(( SECONDS + DEADLINE ))
   714	while builtin :; do
   715	  running=$(builtin jobs -pr)
   716	  case "
   717	$running
   718	" in
   719	    *"
   720	$wid
   721	"*) builtin : ;;             # still running — fall through to the deadline check
   722	    *)
   723	    # Gone from the job table means bash already reaped it and holds its
   724	    # status, so this wait returns immediately. The status is REAPED AND
   725	    # DISCARDED: stderr is closed (see the top of this file), so the parent
   726	    # has nowhere to put a message and deliberately says nothing. The worker
   727	    # exit codes still exist (10-17 documented at the worker, 18 = no
   728	    # interpreter) and every one that can leave a durable trace already wrote
   729	    # a .error.txt before exiting; codes 13, 15 and 18 are the ones that
   730	    # cannot, and those events are silently lost — priced cheap by contract.
   731	    builtin wait "$wid" 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   732	    builtin break
   733	    ;;
   734	  esac
   735	  if builtin [ "$SECONDS" -ge "$end" ]; then
   736	    # Abandon without wait: a worker wedged in an uninterruptible syscall
   737	    # cannot complete SIGKILL, and a wait here would hang the parent on it.
   738	    # The event file, if one was opened, may be left truncated; the reader
   739	    # reports such a file as UNREADABLE.
   740	    builtin kill -KILL "$wid" 2>/dev/null || builtin true
   741	    builtin break
   742	  fi
   743	  # `|| true` IS LOAD-BEARING. The trap at the top of this file catches ERR, and
   744	  # with no `set -e` a BARE failing command still fires it: a /bin/sleep that
   745	  # ever returned non-zero would run `exit 0` here and abandon a live worker
   746	  # without reaching the deadline kill. Verified 2026-08-31: bare fails, guarded
   747	  # continues. Every other command in this loop is already inside if/case/||.
   748	  /bin/sleep 0.05 || builtin true
   749	done
   750	builtin exit 0

codex
Actual source-check results so far: [verified: `/bin/bash -n` exit 0] v22, v26, and v27 parse under both plain and privileged modes. [verified: fresh `diff -u`] the executable v26→v27 change is confined to the probe/worker topology beginning near v27 line 606; [verified: source inspection] the candidate moves `exec 0</dev/null` before probe creation, uses a background `(stat && exec)` probe, closes fd 3 in probes, and later maps fd 3 back to worker stdin.
